ESG Variable Role Summary
ESG VariableAsset SideLiability Side (Asset Share)Discounting
rfr_short(t,s) Bond pricing; reinvestment yield on new fixed income purchases Sets market rate benchmark → drives DPB lapse formula; influences credited rate smoothing Integrated to give discount_factor: \(D(t,s) = e^{-\int_0^t r(u,s)\,du}\)
equity_total_return(t,s) Equity portfolio mark-to-market: \(V^{\text{eq}}(t,s) = V^{\text{eq}}(0)\prod_\tau(1+R^\text{eq}_\tau)\) Feeds earned_rate → higher equity return → higher AS growth → higher bonus potential —
credit_spread(t,s) Corporate bond spread over RFR; wider spread → lower bond price → unrealised loss hits earned rate Indirectly via earned rate; may trigger management action (asset switch) under GL34 —
discount_factor(t,s) — — Folds all liability cash flows back to t=0. Low rates → large discount_factor → high PV of liabilities
fx_rate(t,s) Converts foreign-currency assets to HKD for portfolio valuation Relevant if Par fund holds foreign assets or has foreign policyholders —
Per-Scenario Calculation Path (Steps 1–9)

Step 1 — ESG Input Extraction

For scenario s, extract monthly time series from the ESG output array:
rfr_short[s,t], equity_total_return[s,t], credit_spread[s,t], disc_factor[s,t]
The disc_factor is pre-computed by the ESG as \(D(t,s) = \exp\!\left(-\sum_{\tau=1}^{t} r(\tau,s)\cdot\Delta\tau\right)\) — no further integration needed.

Step 2 — Compute Earned Rate i(t,s)

The earned rate is the total return on the Par fund's asset portfolio in scenario s: \[ i(t,s) = w_{\text{bond}}\cdot\underbrace{\bigl[r^f(t,s) + cs(t,s) - \text{EL} + \Delta P_{\text{MtM}}(t,s)\bigr]}_{\text{bond total return}} + w_{\text{eq}}\cdot R^{\text{eq}}(t,s) + w_{\text{prop}}\cdot R^{\text{prop}}(t,s) \]

Where does EL come from?

EL (Expected Loss) is a static parameter, not from ESG. It is calibrated by the credit team per rating band: \[\text{EL} = \text{PD} \times \text{LGD}\]

RatingPD (annual)LGDEL (typical)
AAA0.01%40%~0.004%
AA0.03%40%~0.012%
A0.08%45%~0.036%
BBB0.20%45%~0.090%
BB1.00%50%~0.500%

Source: Moody's / S&P historical default studies, or internal credit model. EL is subtracted from the credit spread because credit_spread already embeds default compensation — EL removes the expected loss portion, leaving only the risk premium that is actually earned on average.

Where does ΔPMtM come from?

ΔPMtM is derived from the ESG rfr_short and credit_spread paths using the duration approximation: \[ \frac{\Delta P}{P} \approx -D_{\text{mod}} \cdot \Delta y + \tfrac{1}{2}\cdot\text{Conv}\cdot(\Delta y)^2, \qquad \Delta y = \Delta r^f(t,s) + \Delta cs(t,s) \] where \(D_{\text{mod}}\) (modified duration, e.g. 7.5 yrs) and Conv (convexity, e.g. 80) are properties of the existing bond portfolio — not from ESG, but from the asset model. 

# Python: compute MtM contribution per scenario per timestep
delta_y   = (rfr_short[s,t] - rfr_short[s,t-1]) + (credit_spread[s,t] - credit_spread[s,t-1])
D_mod     = 7.5      # portfolio modified duration (from asset model, not ESG)
convexity = 80       # portfolio convexity
MtM       = -D_mod * delta_y + 0.5 * convexity * delta_y**2
# MtM is then added into bond_total_return alongside coupon and reinvestment yield

Practical note: large insurers (e.g. AIA, Prudential) price each bond from the full discounted cash flow, CUSIP (Committee on Uniform Securities Identification Procedures — the unique 9-character identifier for each bond) by CUSIP, rather than using the duration approximation. The approximation is sufficient for ESG-based modelling and scenario analysis.

Step 6 — Cash Flow Projection

Aggregate all outflows and inflows in scenario s, year t:

Cash Flow ItemFormulaScenario Dependency
Death benefitq_death × max(Sum Assured, AS(t,s))AS(t,s) path
Surrender benefitq_lapse(t,s) × max(AS(t,s), SV_floor)AS(t,s) + DPB lapse
Maturity benefitAt T: AS(T,s) + TB(T,s)AS(T,s) path
Premium income−P × (1 − q_lapse(t,s)) × In-force(t,s)DPB lapse reduces in-force
Net CF(t,s)Death + Surrender + Maturity − PremiumAll path-dependent

Step 7 — Discount Cash Flows to t=0

\[ \mathrm{PV}(s) = \sum_{t=1}^{T} CF(t,s) \times D(t,s) \quad\text{where}\quad D(t,s) = e^{-\sum_{\tau=1}^{t} r^f(\tau,s)\cdot\Delta\tau} \] Discount uses the risk-free rate from the ESG (Sch. 4 RFR), not the earned rate — a common mistake. If Matching Adjustment applies, discount rate = RFR + MA; the MA itself may be scenario-dependent (Dynamic MA).

What Goes into CF(t,s)? — Regulatory Basis

Cap. 41R Rule 17(4) defines the scope of cash flows for long term insurance liabilities:

Rule 17(4) — All future cash inflows and outflows … should be included … including but not limited to:
(a) cash inflows from premiums and any other amounts to be received from the policy holders;
(b) cash inflows from non-reinsurance recoveries related to claims;
(c) cash outflows for all types of benefits and payments payable to policy holders and beneficiaries;
(d) cash outflows for expenses incurred in servicing the contract … including investment expenses, claims handling expenses and acquisition expenses.
ItemIn CF(t,s)?Reason
Premium income✅ Yes (inflow, negative sign)Rule 17(4)(a) — direct policyholder cash flow
Death benefit✅ Yes (outflow)Rule 17(4)(c) — benefit payable to policyholder
Surrender value✅ Yes (outflow)Rule 17(4)(c) — benefit payable to policyholder; amount = max(AS, SV_floor) embeds all accumulated returns
Maturity benefit✅ Yes (outflow)Rule 17(4)(c) — AS(T,s) + TB(T,s) already net of tax and expenses via AS recursion
Expenses (E_t)❌ Not separately — embedded in ASRule 17(4)(d) lists expenses, but in the AS model they reduce AS(t,s) in Step 5; the reduced AS is paid out at surrender/maturity, so expenses are implicitly included without double-counting
Investment income❌ NoNot listed in Rule 17(4). Investment income accrues to the fund (asset side), not to policyholders directly. It is embedded in AS growth via earned rate i(t,s) — paying out AS already distributes accumulated investment returns to policyholders
Tax (Tax_t)❌ NoRule 17 (long term) does not list tax as a liability cash flow (unlike Rule 29(4)(e) for general insurance). Tax reduces AS in the recursion (Tax_t term); the after-tax AS is then paid out at surrender/maturity
PROF (profit transfer)❌ NoPROF is a shareholder transfer, not a policyholder cash flow. It reduces AS, but the policyholder's entitlement is to the remaining (post-PROF) AS

Key principle: Investment income, tax and PROF are all internal fund movements — they change the size of AS but are not direct transfers to/from policyholders. By the time a policyholder receives SV or MB, those items are already reflected in the AS amount. Including them again in CF(t,s) would constitute double-counting.

Step 8 — Average Across Scenarios (Stochastic CE)

\[ \mathrm{CE}_{\text{stochastic}} = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{s=1}^{N} \mathrm{PV}(s) \] Typical N = 1,000–5,000 scenarios. Check convergence: plot running mean of PV(s) — if it stabilises by s = 500, N = 1,000 is sufficient. For variance reduction, use antithetic variates: pair each scenario s with its mirror −Z, which halves effective N needed.

Step 9 — TVOG

\[ \mathrm{TVOG} = \mathrm{CE}_{\text{stochastic}} - \mathrm{CE}_{\text{deterministic}} \] The deterministic CE uses a single best-estimate path (IA Sch. 4 forward curve as at valuation date, static lapse = base lapse, no DPB). TVOG is always ≥ 0 by Jensen's inequality. If your model produces negative TVOG, it signals a bug — typically the DPB lapse is moving in the wrong direction, or the discount rate used in deterministic ≠ stochastic runs.

Intuition: Why Low-Rate Scenarios Dominate TVOG
Scenario Typeearned_rate i(t,s)AS GrowthDPB LapsePV of LiabilitiesNet Impact
Low rate (rfr ↓) ↓ (bonds underperform) Slow → bonus cut ↑ (rfr < credited → mass lapse) ↑ (low discount rate) Insurer loss ↑↑
High rate (rfr ↑) ↑ (but reinvestment lag) Fast → bonus rises ↓ (market rate > credited → stay) ↓ (high discount rate) Partial offset
Equity crash ↓↓ (equity drag on earned rate) Very slow / negative ↑ (guarantee binding) Depends on rfr path Guarantee floor activated → TVOG_floor large

The average across all scenarios is always worse than the deterministic best-estimate because of this asymmetry — losses in bad scenarios are not fully offset by gains in good scenarios (Jensen's inequality). This asymmetry is the TVOG.

Deep Dive A: How bond_return Is Built from rfr_short and credit_spread

Many practitioners conflate bond income with bond total return. Under HKRBC EBS (mark-to-market basis), the earned rate captures both cash income and mark-to-market revaluation:

ComponentFormulaESG DriverDirection when rfr ↑
Coupon income Fixed coupon / book value Fixed at purchase; not scenario-driven Unchanged (existing bonds)
Mark-to-market revaluation \(\Delta P \approx -D_{\text{mod}} \times \Delta r \times P\) rfr_short(t,s) + credit_spread(t,s) Negative (bond price falls)
Reinvestment yield New bonds purchased at current rfr + credit spread rfr_short(t,s) + credit_spread(t,s) Positive (higher coupon on new bonds)
Total bond return coupon + Δ price + reinvestment yield Net of all three Negative short-term, positive long-term (duration effect)

This is why a sudden rate rise hurts earned_rate immediately (mark-to-market loss > coupon gain), but gradually recovers as old bonds mature and are replaced at higher yields. The speed of recovery depends on asset duration vs liability duration — the core of ALM.

Deep Dive B: Numerical Walkthrough — One Policy, Two Scenarios, 3 Years

Assumptions: Sum Assured = HKD 100,000 | Annual Premium = HKD 3,000 | Asset mix: 70% bond / 30% equity | Guaranteed rate = 2% | Base lapse = 5% | β = 2.0 | Smoothing: credited rate = 3-year moving average of earned rate

Scenario A — Low Rate (rfr falls to 1%)
Yearrfr_shortequity_returnearned_rate i(t)credited_rateq_lapseAS(t)
t=0—————0
t=11.0%−5.0%0.7×(1.0%−0.5%) + 0.3×(−5.0%) = −1.15%2.5% (lagged smooth)5%×(1+2×max(1%−2.5%,0)) = 5% (no excess)≈ 2,900 − costs ≈ 2,700
t=21.0%+2.0%0.7×0.5% + 0.3×2.0% = 0.95%1.6% (moving avg down)5%×(1+2×max(1%−1.6%,0)) = 5% (no excess)≈ 2,700×1.0095 + 3,000 − costs ≈ 5,626
t=31.0%+3.0%0.7×0.5% + 0.3×3.0% = 1.25%1.07% (avg of −1.15%, 0.95%, 1.25%)5%×(1+2×max(1%−1.07%,0)) = 5%≈ 5,626×1.0125 + 3,000 − costs ≈ 8,696
PV(A) = Σ CF(t,A) × exp(−rfr×t) — surrender value close to AS → low lapse cost here, but discount factor large (low rfr) → PV(A) HIGH
Scenario B — High Rate (rfr rises to 5%)
Yearrfr_shortequity_returnearned_rate i(t)credited_rateq_lapseAS(t)
t=15.0%+8.0%0.7×(5%−2%) + 0.3×8% = 0.7×3%+2.4% = 4.5%2.5% (lagged)5%×(1+2×max(5%−2.5%,0)) = 5%×6 = 30% ← mass lapse!Only 70% of policies remain; AS per survivor ≈ similar but fund shrinks
t=25.0%+6.0%4.3%3.5% (rising)5%×(1+2×max(5%−3.5%,0)) = 20%Fund continues shrinking
t=35.0%+5.0%4.5%4.2%5%×(1+2×max(5%−4.2%,0)) = 13%Surviving policyholders have higher AS
PV(B) = Σ CF(t,B) × exp(−5%×t) — mass lapse triggers large surrender outflows; discount factor small (high rfr) → PV(B) LOWER but surrender cost large

Key insight: In Scenario A, earned_rate turns negative in year 1 — the insurer cannot credit the guaranteed 2%, causing a capital drain. In Scenario B, mass lapse forces the insurer to liquidate assets at market prices (possibly below book) to pay surrender values. The deterministic scenario sits between these two extremes, but the average of the bad outcomes is worse than the midpoint — that gap is TVOG.

Deep Dive C: Python Data Flow — From ESG Array to TVOG
import numpy as np

# ── 输入维度 ──
# esg[variable, scenario, time_step]  shape: (5, N_scen, T)
# variables: 0=rfr_short, 1=equity_return, 2=credit_spread, 3=fx_rate, 4=disc_factor

N_scen, T = 1000, 40          # 1000 情景，投影 40 年

# ── 资产端：earned_rate ──
w_bond, w_equity = 0.70, 0.30
credit_adj = 0.005             # 信用调整（扣除预期违约损失）

# bond_return = rfr + credit_spread - credit_adj + mark-to-market change
# 简化：假设已经从 ESG 提供 bond total return
bond_return   = esg[0] + esg[2] - credit_adj   # shape: (N_scen, T)
equity_return = esg[1]                           # shape: (N_scen, T)

earned_rate = w_bond * bond_return + w_equity * equity_return  # (N_scen, T)

# ── 负债端：Asset Share 递推 ──
AS = np.zeros((N_scen, T+1))   # AS[s, t]
P  = 3000                      # 年保费

# 3 年移动平均 smoothing for credited rate
def smoothed_rate(earned, t, window=3):
    start = max(0, t - window + 1)
    return np.mean(earned[:, start:t+1], axis=1)   # shape: (N_scen,)

beta = 2.0
base_lapse = 0.05
guaranteed_rate = 0.02

CF = np.zeros((N_scen, T))   # 现金流（负债方向：正 = 流出）

for t in range(T):
    i_t       = earned_rate[:, t]               # shape: (N_scen,)
    cred_t    = smoothed_rate(earned_rate, t)   # shape: (N_scen,)
    rfr_t     = esg[0, :, t]                    # shape: (N_scen,)

    # DPB lapse：市场利率超过 credited rate 时超额退保
    excess    = np.maximum(rfr_t - cred_t, 0)
    q_lapse_t = base_lapse * (1 + beta * excess)   # (N_scen,)

    # Asset Share 更新（简化：忽略死亡、费用）
    AS[:, t+1] = AS[:, t] * (1 + i_t) + P

    # 现金流：退保支出 = lapse率 × AS（或 SV floor，取大）
    SV_floor  = 0.85 * AS[:, t+1]    # 假设退保价值 ≥ 85% AS
    surrender_value = np.maximum(AS[:, t+1], SV_floor)
    CF[:, t]  = q_lapse_t * surrender_value

# ── 折现 ──
disc = esg[4]   # discount_factor[s, t] = exp(-∫rfr dt)，ESG 直接输出
PV   = np.sum(CF * disc, axis=1)   # shape: (N_scen,)

# ── TVOG ──
CE_stochastic    = np.mean(PV)
CE_deterministic = run_deterministic_model()   # 单一最优情景的 PV

TVOG = CE_stochastic - CE_deterministic
print(f"TVOG = {TVOG:,.0f} HKD")
print(f"TVOG as % of CE_det = {TVOG/CE_deterministic*100:.2f}%")

§10.2.1 Par Product Structure — Three Liability Layers 三層負債結構

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Layer 3: Terminal Bonus (TB) ─ paid at maturity / surrender / death │  ← Largest, most flexible
  │          Non-vested, fully discretionary                            │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Layer 2: Reversionary Bonus (RB) ─ declared yearly, cumulative      │  ← Once declared → vested
  │          Vested RB becomes guaranteed (cannot be clawed back)       │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Layer 1: Guaranteed Benefit (SA + Guaranteed Cash Value)            │  ← Hard floor
  │          Must be paid regardless of investment performance          │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

TVOG Sources — Multiple Embedded Options

Embedded Option / GuaranteeTriggerBeneficiaryTVOG Sign
Guaranteed SA / Cash ValueInvestment underperformsPolicyholder+ (cost to insurer)
Vested RB cannot be reducedAlways (asymmetric)Policyholder+
Dividend smoothingVolatile marketsBoth (double-edged)± (typically small +)
Surrender with TBPolicyholder chooses optimal exitPolicyholder+
Cap on bonus rateInvestment outperformsInsurer / shareholder− (offset)

Why Par TVOG Is More Complex Than UL TVOG

FeatureUL TVOGPar TVOG
Crediting / dividend rateDeclared by insurer, with floorDeclared with smoothing + GL34-governed discretion
Surrender impactTake Account Value (simple)Triggers TB payout — timing-dependent
Bonus structureSingle crediting rateRB (vested) + TB (discretionary) — asymmetric
Management actionsAnnual rate decisionAnnual RB + periodic TB reset, both documented
Regulatory oversightStandard Rule 19GL34 prescribes management action governance
ESG factor sensitivityPrimarily IRIR + equity + credit spread + FX all material

§10.2.2 Key Modelling Assumptions 關鍵建模假設

(a) Asset Earned Rate 資產回報率投影
\[ i_t = \frac{\text{Investment Income}_t + \text{Realized Gain}_t + \text{Unrealized Gain}_t}{\text{Avg Asset}_t} \]
  • Asset mix: Fixed by ALM strategy (e.g. 70% bond / 20% equity / 10% property)
  • Reinvestment: Specify which curve point new investments use (Sch. 4 RFR + asset spread)
  • Fair value basis: Under HKRBC EBS, all gains (realized + unrealized) enter earned rate
(b) Smoothing Mechanism 平滑機制 (Three Common Methods)
MethodFormulaPros / Cons
Simple Moving Average $$Smoothed_t = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1} Earned_{t-i}$$ (n = 3-10 years) Simple but slow to react
Exponential Smoothing $$Smoothed_t = \alpha \cdot Earned_t + (1-\alpha) \cdot Smoothed_{t-1}$$ (α = 0.2-0.4) Responsive, single parameter
Asset Share Smoothing (industry standard) $$AS_t = AS_{t-1}(1+i_t) + P_t - DB_t - SB_t - E_t - PROF_t$$
Bonus = f(AS, Reserve, Smoothing Buffer)		 Fairest to PRE (Policyholders' Reasonable Expectations); GL34 preferred
(c) Dividend Declaration Rule 分紅宣派規則 (GL34 §6 governed)
# Each year t in each scenario s:
1. free_surplus = Asset(t) - Liability(t) - Min Solvency Capital
2. target_div = long_term_expected_earned_rate - guaranteed_rate
3. actual_div = smoothing(earned_rate, target_div)
4. Apply cap / floor:
     - Floor: RB cannot go negative (0% is the floor); TB can drop to 0
     - Cap: bonus rate cannot exceed earned rate + buffer
5. Split RB vs TB:
     - RB rate = conservative, sustainable portion (e.g. 40% of target)
     - TB rate = surplus-driven portion (e.g. 60% of target)
6. Management buffer: hold back some surplus for future smoothing
(d) RB vs TB Distinction — Critically Important
Bonus TypeVestingReversibilityPath Dependency
Reversionary Bonus (RB) Once declared, immediately vested Cannot be clawed back (becomes guaranteed liability) Cumulative — adds to SA permanently
Terminal Bonus (TB) Paid only at maturity / surrender / death Fully adjustable any time before payout Path-dependent — value depends on surrender timing
(e) Dynamic Policyholder Behaviour with TB Anticipation
\[ q^{\text{lapse}}_t = q^{\text{base}} \times \left(1 + \beta_1\cdot\max(r^{\text{mkt}} - r^{\text{credited}},\,0) + \beta_2\cdot\max\!\left(\frac{TB_t - TB_{t+1}^{\text{exp}}}{TB_t},\,0\right) \right) \]

The second term captures TB anticipation effect — policyholders surrender early to lock in current TB level if they expect TB to decline. β1 ≈ 1.5-3.0, β2 ≈ 1.0-2.0 (calibrated from historical experience).

§10.2.3 End-to-End Calculation Process 完整計算流程 (10 Steps)

StepActionOutput
1Prepare ESG scenarios (≥1,000 risk-neutral, covering IR/equity/credit/FX)Scenario matrix
2 For each scenario s, year t, project:
(a) Asset value roll-forward
(b) Earned rate calculation
(c) Apply smoothing → smoothed rate
(d) Apply dividend rule → RB rate, TB rate
(e) Update vested RB (accumulates to liability)
(f) Compute TB reserve (based on current expected surplus)
(g) Apply DPB-adjusted lapse (with TB anticipation)
(h) Compute all CFs: premium, claim, surrender, expense
(i) Pay TB at surrender / death / maturity events 		Year-by-year CF
3Discount all CFs to t=0 using scenario s's RFR path
PVs = Σ CFt × DFt		PV per scenario
4Stochastic CE = (1/N) × Σ PVsCEstoch
5Deterministic CE: re-run the same projection ONCE using ESG mean-path scenarioCEdet
6TVOGPar = CEstoch − CEdetTVOG figure
Optional Attribution (Steps 7-10): decompose TVOG sources
7Re-run without floors → isolate TVOGfloor (guarantee cost)Guarantee TVOG
8Re-run without smoothing → isolate TVOGsmoothingSmoothing TVOG
9Re-run without dynamic surrender → isolate TVOGsurrenderSurrender option TVOG
10Re-run without cap → isolate TVOGcap (offset)Cap benefit

§10.2.4 Asset Share Model & GL34 Compliance 資產份額模型與GL34合規 GL34 (effective 1 Jul 2024)

ℹ️ AS 遞推公式（架構、各項定義、SB 計算方法、AS(0) 初始化）詳見 §9 Asset Share（§9.1–§9.4）。 本節專注於 GL34 合規視角：每個公式項目的法規依據與審計問題。

AS 遞推公式（§9.2）： \(AS_t = AS_{t-1}(1+i_t) + P_t - DB_t - SB_t - E_t - C_t - \mathrm{Tax}_t - \mathrm{PROF}_t\)

下表列出每個項目對應的 GL34 條款與審計核心問題：

SymbolMeaning 意義GL34 Reference 對應條款Key Audit Question
AS0 Opening asset share at policy inception (or fund effective date) GL34 §4 Opening Balance Is it ≥ amount attributable on day before effective date? (§4.1)
Are there any asymmetric distributions since 1 Jan 2019? (§4.2)
it Asset earned rate (investment return on the fund's physically segregated assets) GL34 §3, §8 Identification & Physical Segregation Are the underlying assets correctly identified and segregated per the fund (§3, §8.1)?
For sub-HKD 1B funds: earmarked properly? (§8.2-§8.3)
Pt Premium income (including riders if material per §3.3) GL34 §3.3 Are riders & FOD properly classified per substance over form? (§3.3)
DBt, SBt Death benefit, surrender benefit cash outflows GL34 §3.4 Properly tracked in books of account per §22 of Ordinance?
Et Expenses allocated to the fund (acquisition, maintenance, investment) GL34 §5 Expenses & Charges Allocation fair, equitable, reasonable (§5.1)? Cost benefits policyholders (§5.2)?
Related-party at arm's length (§5.3)? Inflated charges treated as profit distribution (§5.4)?
Ct Cost of insurance (mortality / morbidity charges) GL34 §5.5 Reflects best estimate or contractual basis?
Taxt Tax attributable to the fund GL34 §5 Allocated based on fund's actual tax position?
PROFt Shareholder profit charge (the "90/10 split" or equivalent mechanism) GL34 §6 Allocation of Distributable Surplus/Profits Fair balance of risk & reward (§6.3 footnote 4)?
Not accelerating shareholder distribution asymmetrically (§6.5)?
Consistently applied year-to-year (§6.7)?
(b) Three Asset Share Methods 三種計算方法
Method 方法Description 描述Strengths / Weaknesses
Retrospective Asset Share
追溯式		 Roll forward AS from policy inception using actual historical earned rates, expenses, claims, and PROF charges ✅ Most accurate; reflects actual experience
✅ GL34 §4 Opening Balance auditable
❌ Data-intensive (need 20-30 yrs history); legacy systems may not have records
Prospective Asset Share
前瞻式		 Project AS forward using best-estimate assumptions; calibrate to current liability value ✅ Quick; useful for new business pricing
❌ Does not reflect historical fund performance
❌ Not suitable for GL34 §4 audit
Hybrid / Sub-fund Asset Share
混合 / 子基金式		 Retrospective at cohort/sub-fund level instead of per-policy; pool-level AS allocated to cohorts via attribution rules ✅ Industry standard for HK Par funds
✅ Balances accuracy with operational feasibility
✅ Aligns with GL34 §2.3 "granularity fit for purpose"
(c) Dividend Declaration Driven by Asset Share GL34合規的分紅決策流程

The IA expects insurers to declare dividends based on Asset Share — not on arbitrary management judgement. The standard process under GL34 §6:

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Step 1: Calculate ASt per cohort (retrospective rollforward)         │
  │         per §3, §4, §5, §6 cost & profit allocation rules        │
  │                              ↓                                  │
  │ Step 2: Calculate Guaranteed Liability (GLt) per cohort          │
  │         under Cap. 41R Rule 16 (current estimate, no bonus)    │
  │                              ↓                                  │
  │ Step 3: Surplust = ASt − GLt − Smoothing Buffer            │
  │         (Smoothing Buffer reserved for future fair payout)      │
  │                              ↓                                  │
  │ Step 4: Apply 90/10 (or fund-specific) profit split             │
  │         Distributable to PH = Surplus × PH_share               │
  │                              ↓                                  │
  │ Step 5: Split into RB (vested, sustainable) and TB              │
  │         RB ≤ amount that fund can sustainably support           │
  │         TB = residual + surplus buffer release                  │
  │                              ↓                                  │
  │ Step 6: Appointed Actuary recommends to Board (§6.8)            │
  │         Board approves bonus declaration (§1.5)                 │
  │                              ↓                                  │
  │ Step 7: Document basis in board policy (§6.7); apply            │
  │         consistently year-to-year (§6.7); IA report (§6.6)      │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
(d) TVOG via Asset Share Decomposition

The economic value of the Par fund liability can be expressed in terms of Asset Share:

\[ V_{\text{Par}} = \underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[AS_T \cdot D_T\right]}_{\text{terminal AS payout}} +\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\max(GL_T - AS_T,\,0)\cdot D_T\right]}_{\text{guarantee floor (put on AS)}} +\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\text{early exercise value}\right]}_{\text{surrender option (American put)}} \]
\[ \mathrm{TVOG}_{\text{Par}} = V_{\text{Par}} - \mathrm{CE}_{\text{deterministic}} \]

This decomposition is powerful because it maps cleanly onto closed-form approximations (Black-Scholes put for the guarantee, Bermudan put for the surrender option), allowing fast TVOG estimation when full stochastic runs are infeasible.

(e) Asset Share Numerical Example 數值範例
ItemYear 1Year 2Year 3...Year 20 (Maturity)
Opening AS010,00020,500...250,000
+ Premium10,00010,00010,000...10,000
+ Investment income (5%)5001,0001,525...13,000
− Expenses (acquisition Y1; maintenance Y2+)−400−100−105...−200
− Cost of insurance−50−55−60...−150
− Shareholder profit charge (10%)−50−100−155...−1,300
Closing AS10,00020,50031,705...270,000
Guaranteed Liability9,00018,50028,500...200,000
Distributable Surplus = AS − GL1,0002,0003,205...70,000 → Bonuses

At maturity, the 70,000 distributable surplus is paid out as accumulated RB + TB. Under stochastic ESG, AS20 varies by scenario; the average payout drives stochastic CE, while Jensen's inequality (due to the floor at GL20 = 200,000) drives positive TVOG.

(f) GL34 Compliance Audit Trail 合規審計鏈條
GL34 RequirementHow Asset Share Demonstrates Compliance
§1.3 PH should not bear undisclosed disadvantage AS calculation is fully transparent; PROF charge explicitly identified per §5.4
§3 Identification of assets & liabilities AS is calculated per identified fund, using identified fund's earned rate
§4 Opening balance sufficiency AS0 = day-1 attributable amount; audited by Independent Professional (§9)
§4.2 Pre-2019 asymmetric distributions AS rollforward starts from 1 Jan 2019; any one-off shareholder distribution flagged
§5 Fair, equitable, reasonable expense allocation Et in AS formula uses transparent allocation keys (e.g., premium-based, policy-count-based)
§5.4 Inflated charges = profit distribution If actual cost < charged amount, the excess flows to PROFt (not to Et)
§6.1 Allocation fair & equitable, in line with PRE, sustainable Bonus declaration tied directly to AS surplus (not arbitrary)
§6.4 Balance between exiting and continuing policyholders Smoothing Buffer maintained in AS framework; TB calibrated against fund's projected surplus
§6.5 No asymmetric acceleration to shareholders PROFt follows fixed percentage rule (e.g. 10%); ad-hoc transfers flagged
§6.7 Consistent application year-to-year AS methodology documented in board policy; changes require board re-approval
§6.8 Annual Appointed Actuary report AS calculation forms the actuarial basis for the AA's bonus declaration recommendation
§8 Physical segregation (≥ HKD 1B funds) AS uses earned rate from segregated custodian/bank accounts (§8.4)
§9 Independent Professional report (by 31 Mar 2025) AS rollforward, opening balance, expense allocation, profit split all subject to independent review
(g) Asset Share & Cohort Segmentation 隊列分組

In production, Asset Share is computed per cohort, typically defined by:

  • Issue year (vintage)
  • Product code (e.g. WL with-profits 1995, Endowment 2010)
  • Currency (HKD / USD / RMB)
  • Distribution channel (if profit-sharing varies)
  • Asset pool (if multiple sub-funds exist within one Par fund per §8.1)

Cohort segmentation is critical for GL34 §6.4 "balance between exiting and continuing policyholders" — without cohort-level AS, you cannot demonstrate that old policies are not subsidising new business (or vice versa).

(h) Common Asset Share Pitfalls 常見計算陷阱
PitfallGL34 RiskMitigation
Using fund total / number of policies instead of true per-cohort AS§6.4 violation: cross-cohort subsidyBuild cohort-level AS with proper attribution keys
Acquisition cost (Y1) recovered too slowly§6.5 risk: artificial PH AS suppressionUse industry-standard amortisation period (e.g. 10 yrs)
Profit charge (PROFt) front-loaded§5.4, §6.5: asymmetric accelerationUse level percentage of premium or fund value
No allowance for inflated expense charges§5.4: profit not properly identifiedReconcile actual vs charged expense; difference → PROF
Treating reinsurance recoveries as part of AS earned rate§5: distorts true investment returnRI recoveries net of premium ceded handled separately
Ignoring tax on fund§5: under-allocation of costAllocate tax based on fund's actual tax position
Changing methodology without board approval§6.7: inconsistent applicationAll AS methodology changes require board re-approval & AA opinion

§10.2.5 Stochastic-on-Stochastic (Nested MC) 雙重隨機

When Required: If management action depends on current fair value (not just current earned rate), the inner loop must re-value liability at every outer point — leading to nested MC (1,000 × 1,000 = 1,000,000 simulations per policy).

Common workarounds to avoid nested MC computational explosion:

  • Least-Squares Monte Carlo (LSMC, Longstaff-Schwartz): Regress continuation values on state variables; replaces inner MC with polynomial fit. Industry standard for VA hedging and complex Par.
  • Replicating Portfolio (RP): Find a portfolio of vanilla options that replicates liability cash flows. Once calibrated, RP can be valued in closed form at any state.
  • Proxy Models / Curve Fitting / Gaussian Process Regression: Build a fast surrogate for inner valuation using ML techniques.

§10.2.6 TVOG Component Decomposition 來源拆解

\[ \mathrm{TVOG}_{\text{Par}} = \mathrm{TVOG}_{\text{floor}} - \mathrm{TVOG}_{\text{cap}} + \mathrm{TVOG}_{\text{smoothing}} + \mathrm{TVOG}_{\text{surrender}} \]
ComponentNatureTypical SignMagnitude Driver
TVOGfloorCost of guaranteed minimum (put option to PH)+ (large)Interest rate level, asset volatility
TVOGcapBenefit of bonus cap (call option to insurer)− (offset)Cap level, asset upside vol
TVOGsmoothingLong-term cost of dividend smoothing+ (small but persistent)Smoothing parameter, asset volatility
TVOGsurrender optionPH selects optimal exit timing for TB+ (medium)TB volatility, surrender penalty, β2

§10.2.7 Asset-Liability Feedback Loop 資產負債回饋循環

Par fund TVOG calculation has a unique feedback loop not present in other product TVOG calculations. The chain runs as follows in each stress scenario: 

  IR Down stress
    → Asset value ↑ (bonds rally)
    → Guaranteed liability ↑ (lower discount rate)
    → Fund surplus change is ambiguous
    → Affects current year's dividend declaration
    → Affects subsequent RB / TB pattern
    → Affects policyholder surrender behaviour (via DPB)
    → Feeds back to asset rebalancing decision
    → Asset duration shifts
    → Repeats next year

This feedback must be modelled sequentially within each scenario — no shortcuts allowed. Locking dividends at base-case levels and running stress separately leads to material TVOG misstatement.

§10.2.8 GL34 Impact on Par TVOG GL34對分紅TVOG的影響 GL34, effective 1 Jul 2024

GL34 RequirementTVOG Impact
§3 Asset & Liability IdentificationPar fund must be physically segregated; TVOG only uses this fund's asset return — cannot pool with non-Par
§4 Asset Share FairnessDividend declaration rule must be fair to policyholders; asset share calculation is the audit basis
§5 Expenses & ChargesCost allocation to par fund must be fair, equitable, reasonable — affects asset earned rate
§6 Bonus Philosophy DocumentManagement action rule must be board-approved and consistent over time — can't be tweaked to reduce TVOG opportunistically
§7 PRE (Policyholders' Reasonable Expectations)TVOG calculation must reflect PRE — overly aggressive management actions challenged by IA
§8 Independent Actuarial ReviewMaterial TVOG changes require independent actuary sign-off
Eligibility for MAPhysical segregation under GL34 is a precondition for Par fund's MA portfolio eligibility under Cap. 41R Rule 24(3) — and for qualified LTA

§10.2.9 Simplified Python Implementation 簡化Python實作

import numpy as np

def par_fund_tvog(scenarios, policy, smoothing_alpha=0.3,
                  rb_share=0.4, tb_share=0.6, beta1=2.0, beta2=1.5):
    """Compute Par fund TVOG (simplified illustration).
    
    scenarios:  np.array shape (N, T), per-year earned rate
    policy:     dict with SA, guaranteed_rate, base_lapse, qx[], etc.
    """
    N, T = scenarios.shape
    pv_per_scenario = np.zeros(N)
    
    for s in range(N):
        # Initialize
        asset = policy['initial_asset']
        vested_rb = 0  # cumulative RB (vested → guaranteed)
        smoothed_rate = scenarios[s, 0]
        last_inforce = 1.0
        cf_per_year = np.zeros(T)
        
        for t in range(T):
            earned_t = scenarios[s, t]
            
            # ─── Smoothing ───
            smoothed_rate = (smoothing_alpha * earned_t
                             + (1 - smoothing_alpha) * smoothed_rate)
            
            # ─── Dividend declaration (with cap/floor) ───
            target_div = max(smoothed_rate - policy['guaranteed_rate'], 0)
            target_div = min(target_div, policy['div_cap'])
            
            # ─── Split into RB (vested) and TB (discretionary) ───
            rb_rate_t = rb_share * target_div
            tb_rate_t = tb_share * target_div
            vested_rb += rb_rate_t * policy['SA']
            
            # ─── DPB with TB anticipation ───
            rate_gap = max(scenarios[s, t] - smoothed_rate, 0)
            tb_decline = max((tb_rate_t - tb_share * target_div), 0)  # proxy
            lapse_t = policy['base_lapse'] * (1 + beta1 * rate_gap + beta2 * tb_decline)
            
            # ─── Cash flows ───
            death_outflow = (policy['SA'] + vested_rb) * policy['qx'][t]
            surr_outflow = ((policy['SA'] * policy['guaranteed_cv_pct']
                             + vested_rb
                             + tb_rate_t * policy['SA'])
                            * lapse_t)
            
            cf_t = -(death_outflow + surr_outflow) * last_inforce
            cf_per_year[t] = cf_t
            
            last_inforce *= (1 - policy['qx'][t] - lapse_t)
            asset = asset * (1 + earned_t) + cf_t
        
        # ─── Discount using scenario's risk-free path ───
        df = np.cumprod(1 / (1 + scenarios[s, :]))
        pv_per_scenario[s] = np.sum(cf_per_year * df)
    
    ce_stochastic = pv_per_scenario.mean()
    
    # Deterministic CE: use mean path (single run)
    mean_path = scenarios.mean(axis=0, keepdims=True)
    ce_deterministic, _, _ = par_fund_tvog(mean_path, policy, smoothing_alpha,
                                            rb_share, tb_share, beta1, beta2)
    
    tvog = ce_stochastic - ce_deterministic
    return ce_stochastic, ce_deterministic, tvog

Note: production code is typically 10-50x more complex (handles multi-currency, cohort segmentation, full asset modelling, replicating portfolio shortcuts for nested MC, etc.).

§10.2.10 Common Pitfalls Specific to Par TVOG 分紅TVOG常見陷阱

PitfallConsequenceSolution
Smoothing buffer too generous TVOG artificially suppressed; IA challenge Buffer parameter validated against historical bonus history
Arbitrary RB / TB split (e.g. 50/50) TVOG result unstable across reporting periods Board-approved split rule, documented in bonus policy
Asset share mis-calculation Systematic TVOG bias Model AS per cohort separately; reconcile to fund total
Overly aggressive management action GL34 violation; PRE breach Back-test against 5-10 yrs actual management decisions
Surrender model ignores TB anticipation Significant TVOG under-estimation DPB rule must include β2 term on TB trajectory
Cap / floor set unreasonably TVOG exhibits step changes across scenarios Sensitivity test for continuity across cap/floor boundary
Cohort aggregation Old vs new policies mixed; misleading TVOG Run TVOG per cohort, then aggregate; cohort = (issue year, product code, fund)
Skipping ALM feedback loop TVOG materially mis-stated Strict time-step sequencing — no parallel shortcuts
Ignoring fund-level constraints Capital relief overstated Apply fund solvency floor to dividend declaration rule

§10.2.11 Production Considerations 實務生產要點

AspectTypical Setup
Model granularityPer cohort × per fund × per currency (often 20-100 segments)
Stochastic runs5,000-10,000 scenarios (Par needs more than UL due to multiple sources)
ToolsProphet ALS (Par module) / AXIS / RiskAgility ESG mode; replicating portfolio via internal Python
Run time1-2 days per fund per quarter on grid (50-100 nodes)
ValidationConvergence test + attribution (Step 7-10) + back-testing against actual bonus declarations
Sign-offChief Actuary + Independent Actuary review (GL34 §8) + Board (bonus declaration aspect)

§10.3 Par TVOG in Prophet — End-to-End Production Workflow 分紅TVOG在Prophet的完整實作工作流

This section translates the Asset Share TVOG methodology (§10.2.4) into a concrete end-to-end Prophet production workflow. Prophet (by FIS / Sungard) is the dominant actuarial modelling platform in Hong Kong, used by AIA, Prudential, AXA, Manulife, China Life and others. The workflow below covers all 7 phases from upstream data to final IA-ready TVOG report.

§10.3.1 Workflow Overview 7-Phase Pipeline

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Phase 1: Data Preparation 數據準備                                    │
  │   PAS extract → MPF (Model Point File)                              │
  │   Custodian / Bloomberg → Asset File                                 │
  │   Assumption updates (mort, lapse, expense)                          │
  │   ESG calibration → Scenario File (.GSI / .ECN)                     │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Phase 2: Prophet Library & Config Setup 模型配置                     │
  │   Library version (e.g. PROPHET_HK_PAR_v25.1)                       │
  │   Asset Share variable code                                          │
  │   Dividend declaration rule code                                     │
  │   Run setting (.RUN file) — stochastic mode                          │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Phase 3: Pre-Run Validation 開跑前驗證                                │
  │   Single-scenario sanity check                                       │
  │   Static validation (assets ≈ liabilities at t=0)                    │
  │   Reconcile vs prior quarter                                         │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Phase 4: Deterministic Run 確定式跑                                   │
  │   Run with ESG mean-path scenario only (1 scenario)                  │
  │   Output: CE_deterministic per cohort                                │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Phase 5: Stochastic Production Run 隨機式跑 (Grid Computing)         │
  │   1,000-5,000 scenarios × 100 years × all cohorts                    │
  │   Parallel on Pacer Grid (50-100 nodes); 12-36 hours                 │
  │   Output: PV per scenario per cohort                                 │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Phase 6: Post-Processing & TVOG Calc 後處理                          │
  │   CE_stochastic = mean(PV_s); TVOG = CE_stoch − CE_det               │
  │   Attribution: TVOG_floor / cap / smoothing / surrender              │
  │   Convergence test (N=1000 vs N=5000)                                │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Phase 7: Validation & GL34 Reporting 驗證與監管報告                  │
  │   AA recommendation memo                                             │
  │   Independent Actuary review (GL34 §8)                               │
  │   IA quarterly filing + Board reporting                              │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

§10.3.2 Phase 1: Data Preparation 數據準備

Input TypeSourceProphet FormatKey Validation
Model Point File (MPF) PAS (Policy Admin System) extract .RPT or .PRO file: one row per cohort with all policy attributes (SA, premium, vested RB, issue date, etc.) Total inforce SA, premium, count reconciles to PAS GL totals; asset share per cohort must rollforward correctly from last period
Asset Data Custodian feed + Bloomberg AAPI Asset MPF: bond CFs, ratings, market value, asset_spread, asset_duration (per §9.2) Total MV reconciles to custodian; spread spot-check vs Bloomberg YAS
Best Estimate Assumptions Annual experience study + management overlay .TAB files (mortality, lapse base, expense per region, COI rates) Sign-off by Appointed Actuary
ESG Scenarios Moody's B&H / Conning GEMS / WTW STAR .GSI or .ECN scenario file: rates, equity, credit spread, FX per year per scenario 5-test validation (martingale / PV / market / moment / distribution) per §9.5
Dividend Policy Parameters Board-approved bonus philosophy doc (GL34 §6.7) Variables in library: smoothing_alpha, rb_share, tb_share, cap, floor Same as last quarter unless board re-approval
Opening Asset Share Prior quarter MPF rollforward + independent reconciliation (GL34 §4) Field in MPF: OPEN_AS per cohort Reconciles to fund GL net of last period bonus declaration

§10.3.3 Phase 2: Prophet Library Setup & Asset Share Variable Code 庫設置與變量代碼

The Asset Share calculation in Prophet is implemented as a set of user-defined variables in the library (.LIB file). Below is a representative pseudo-code skeleton in Prophet's variable language:

'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
' VARIABLE: ASSET_SHARE
' Type: Numeric, Aggregation = Sum, Output Level = Cohort × Scenario × Year
'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
ASSET_SHARE = IF (t = 1) THEN
                  OPEN_AS                  ' from MPF input field
              ELSE
                  ASSET_SHARE_PRV * (1 + EARNED_RATE_NET)
                  + PREMIUM_INCOME
                  - DEATH_OUTGO
                  - SURR_OUTGO
                  - EXPENSE_ALLOCATED
                  - COI_CHARGE
                  - TAX_FUND
                  - PROFIT_CHARGE_SH        ' shareholder transfer per §6
              END IF

'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
' VARIABLE: EARNED_RATE_NET (driven by ESG scenario s)
'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
EARNED_RATE_NET = (INV_INC + REALIZED_GL + UNREALIZED_GL) / AVG_ASSET
                  - INV_EXPENSE_RATE
   ' Driven by ESG scenario through ASSET_RETURN_S(t, s) lookup

'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
' VARIABLE: SMOOTHED_RATE (5-year exponential)
'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
SMOOTHED_RATE = IF (t = 1) THEN
                    EARNED_RATE_NET
                ELSE
                    SMOOTHING_ALPHA * EARNED_RATE_NET
                    + (1 - SMOOTHING_ALPHA) * SMOOTHED_RATE_PRV
                END IF

'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
' VARIABLE: TOTAL_BONUS_RATE (GL34 §6 governed declaration rule)
'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
DISTRIBUTABLE = MAX(ASSET_SHARE - GTD_LIAB - SMOOTHING_BUFFER, 0)
TARGET_RATE   = MIN(SMOOTHED_RATE - GTD_RATE, DIVIDEND_CAP)
TOTAL_BONUS_RATE = MAX(0, MIN(TARGET_RATE,
                              DISTRIBUTABLE / SA_INFORCE))

'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
' VARIABLE: RB_RATE_T, TB_RATE_T  (RB vested, TB discretionary)
'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
RB_RATE_T = RB_SHARE_PCT * TOTAL_BONUS_RATE         ' e.g. 40%
TB_RATE_T = TB_SHARE_PCT * TOTAL_BONUS_RATE         ' e.g. 60%

'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
' VARIABLE: VESTED_RB_BAL  (cumulative — once declared, vested)
'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
VESTED_RB_BAL = IF (t = 1) THEN
                    OPEN_VESTED_RB
                ELSE
                    VESTED_RB_BAL_PRV + RB_RATE_T * SA_INFORCE
                END IF

'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
' VARIABLE: DYNAMIC_LAPSE_RATE (DPB with TB anticipation)
'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
RATE_GAP = MAX(MARKET_RATE - SMOOTHED_RATE, 0)
TB_DECLINE_SIG = MAX((TB_RATE_T_PRV - TB_RATE_T) / TB_RATE_T_PRV, 0)
DYNAMIC_LAPSE_RATE = BASE_LAPSE_TABLE * (1 + BETA1 * RATE_GAP
                                            + BETA2 * TB_DECLINE_SIG)

'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
' VARIABLE: CASH FLOWS for the cohort in this scenario-year
'═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
DEATH_OUTGO = (SA_INFORCE + VESTED_RB_BAL) * MORTALITY_RATE
SURR_OUTGO  = (SA_INFORCE * GTD_CV_PCT
               + VESTED_RB_BAL
               + TB_RATE_T * SA_INFORCE)      ' TB paid at surrender
              * DYNAMIC_LAPSE_RATE
MATURITY_OUTGO = IF (t = POL_TERM) THEN
                     SA_INFORCE + VESTED_RB_BAL + TB_RATE_T * SA_INFORCE
                 ELSE 0 END IF

Real Prophet code uses Prophet-specific syntax (e.g., PRJ_ prefix, RPT_ variables, LOOKUP functions); this is a methodology illustration. A full production library can have 200-500 such variables.

§10.3.4 Phase 3: Pre-Run Validation 開跑前驗證

ValidationMethodAcceptance Criterion
Single-scenario sanity check Run 1 scenario, eyeball CF projection CFs reasonable, no negative claims, asset depletes monotonically
Static balance test At t=0: AS0 should reconcile with prior period closing AS + bonus declared Difference < 0.1% of fund value
Roll-forward test Compare current quarter MPF to prior quarter MPF + 1 quarter rollforward Movements explained: premium, claims, bonus, FX, model changes
Cohort coverage Sum of cohort SA should reconcile to fund total SA < 0.01% discrepancy
ESG scenarios load test Sample 5 scenarios; check rate paths plausible No negative rates beyond model floor; equity paths within ESG calibrated bounds
Library version control Git diff vs last quarter's library All code changes signed off by AA

§10.3.5 Phase 4 & 5: Deterministic + Stochastic Production Runs 確定式與隨機式

Phase 4 — Deterministic Run (1 scenario, fast)

  • Input: ESG mean path scenario file (single .GSI with 1 scenario containing the per-timepoint expected value)
  • Output: CEdeterministic per cohort, per fund, per currency
  • Run time: ~30 min on single node for 500K policies
  • Purpose: Baseline for TVOG denominator; also used as "best estimate CE" in PCR base run

Phase 5 — Stochastic Production Run (N scenarios, slow)

ConfigurationTypical SetupNotes
Scenarios1,000 (minimum) or 5,000 (industry standard)Variance reduction: antithetic + Sobol
Projection horizon100 years (or until last policy maturity)Annual or quarterly time steps
Compute platformProphet Pacer Grid (50-100 nodes)Distributed across cohorts × scenarios
Run time12-36 hours per quarter per fundScales linearly with scenario count
OutputPVs per scenario, per cohort, per cash flow typeStored in Prophet results database (RDF)
Output size500K policies × 5K scenarios ≈ 200-500 GB rawCompressed and archived for audit

Sample Prophet Run Configuration File (.RUN)

[Run Settings]
RUN_NAME            = HKRBC_Q4_2025_PARFUND_STOCH_5K
RUN_TYPE            = STOCHASTIC
SCENARIO_FILE       = ESG_MoodysBH_Q4_2025_5000scn.GSI
SCENARIOS           = 5000
ANTITHETIC          = ON
PROJECTION_YEARS    = 100
TIME_STEP           = MONTHLY
MODEL_POINT_FILE    = HKD_PAR_FUND_A_2025Q4.RPT
LIBRARY             = PROPHET_HK_PAR_v25.4
TABLE_DIR           = .\TABLES\2025Q4\
DISCOUNT_BASIS      = RFR_SCENARIO            ' use per-scenario RFR path
OUTPUT_LEVEL        = COHORT, SCENARIO, YEAR
OUTPUT_VARIABLES    = ASSET_SHARE, GTD_LIAB, VESTED_RB_BAL, &
                      DEATH_OUTGO, SURR_OUTGO, MATURITY_OUTGO, &
                      DISCOUNT_FACTOR, PV_CF

[Distributed Computing]
GRID                = PACER_HK_PROD_50NODES
SPLIT_BY            = SCENARIO
RESTART_ON_FAIL     = ON

§10.3.6 Phase 6: Post-Processing & TVOG Calculation 後處理

Step-by-step Post-Processing

StepOperationOutput
1 Extract PVs from Prophet RDF, one row per (cohort, scenario) Tidy DataFrame
2 Stochastic CE per cohort = mean(PVs over all scenarios) CEstoch per cohort
3 Deterministic CE per cohort = PV from Phase 4 run CEdet per cohort
4 TVOG per cohort = CEstoch − CEdet TVOG figure
5 Aggregate cohort TVOGs to fund total Fund-level TVOG
6 Standard error: SE = std(PVs) / √N per cohort SE metrics
7 Convergence test: re-aggregate using first N=1000 only; compare to N=5000 Convergence ratio
8 Attribution: re-run with one option turned off at a time (floor / cap / smoothing / surrender) TVOG components
9 Sensitivity: ±10% lapse, ±50 bps RFR, ±20% equity vol Sensitivity grid

Sample Python Post-Processing Script

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

# ── Load Prophet output (extracted from RDF) ──
df_stoch = pd.read_parquet('PROPHET_RDF/HKRBC_Q4_2025_STOCH_5K.parquet')
# Columns: cohort_id, scenario, year, pv_cf

df_det = pd.read_parquet('PROPHET_RDF/HKRBC_Q4_2025_DET_MEAN.parquet')
# Columns: cohort_id, year, pv_cf

# ── Step 1-2: Stochastic CE per cohort ──
ce_stoch = (df_stoch.groupby(['cohort_id', 'scenario'])['pv_cf'].sum()
                    .groupby('cohort_id').mean())

# ── Step 3: Deterministic CE per cohort ──
ce_det = df_det.groupby('cohort_id')['pv_cf'].sum()

# ── Step 4: TVOG per cohort ──
tvog = ce_stoch - ce_det
tvog_pct = tvog / ce_det * 100

# ── Step 5: Fund-level TVOG ──
fund_tvog = tvog.sum()
fund_tvog_pct = fund_tvog / ce_det.sum() * 100

# ── Step 6: Standard error per cohort ──
pv_by_scen = df_stoch.groupby(['cohort_id', 'scenario'])['pv_cf'].sum()
se = pv_by_scen.groupby('cohort_id').std() / np.sqrt(5000)
relative_se = se / ce_stoch.abs() * 100

# ── Step 7: Convergence test ──
ce_stoch_1k = (pv_by_scen.unstack().iloc[:, :1000].mean(axis=1))
convergence_diff = (ce_stoch - ce_stoch_1k) / ce_stoch * 100

# ── Build summary report ──
report = pd.DataFrame({
    'CE_Deterministic': ce_det,
    'CE_Stochastic':    ce_stoch,
    'TVOG':             tvog,
    'TVOG_%':           tvog_pct,
    'StdErr_%':         relative_se,
    'Conv_Diff_%':      convergence_diff,
}).round(2)

report.to_excel('TVOG_Q4_2025_Summary.xlsx')

print(f"Fund-level TVOG: HKD {fund_tvog/1e6:.1f}M ({fund_tvog_pct:.2f}% of CE)")
print(f"Average cohort SE: {relative_se.mean():.2f}%")
print(f"Max cohort convergence diff: {convergence_diff.abs().max():.2f}%")

§10.3.7 Phase 7: Validation & GL34 Reporting 驗證與監管報告

Output DeliverableAudienceGL34 / Cap. 41R Reference
TVOG Calculation Memo (methodology, assumptions, results, attribution) Appointed Actuary, Audit, IA Cap. 41R Rule 19; IA Circular App. 1
Asset Share Rollforward Reconciliation (prior CE → CE_stoch this period) AA, Independent Actuary, Audit GL34 §4 (Opening Balance), §6.6 (tracking)
Bonus Declaration Recommendation (from AS surplus) Appointed Actuary → Board GL34 §6.8

§10.3.8 Common Production Issues 常見生產問題

IssueRoot CauseMitigation
Grid run hangs / fails midway Memory leak, node failure, ESG scenario disk I/O Restart-on-fail flag, split scenarios into smaller chunks (e.g. 500 per batch)
Stochastic CE materially different from prior quarter ESG re-calibration, model code change, MPF data shift Run "change explainer" — turn changes on one-at-a-time, isolate driver
Asset Share opening balance mismatch Cohort restructure / new business added without proper roll Independent reconciliation against custodian + AA approval
TVOG too high vs benchmark Overly conservative DPB; insufficient management action; small smoothing buffer Sensitivity test on β2 (TB anticipation), validate management action against historical
TVOG too low vs benchmark Overly aggressive management action; missing surrender option modelling GL34 §6 PRE check; back-test management action against last 5 years
Convergence diff > 5% Insufficient scenarios for deep out-of-the-money guarantees Increase N to 10,000 or apply importance sampling for tail
Negative Asset Share in stressed scenarios Adverse path triggers severe reserve buildup > AS Capital support tracking (GL34 §7); AS floor at 0 in PROF charge
Library code change unapproved Developer mid-quarter fix without proper version control Git lockdown during production run; AA must sign off any code change

§11 ALM & HKRBC Linkage 資產負債管理與香港風險資本的關聯

Asset-Liability Management (ALM 資產負債管理) and HKRBC are tightly interlocked: how well a company manages its ALM directly determines the size of its Matching Adjustment, the magnitude of its Interest Rate and Credit Spread PCR, and the credibility of its LAC(FDB). Good ALM practice is not just about investment strategy — under HKRBC, it translates quantitatively into capital savings.

ALM Decision 資產負債管理決策HKRBC Impact 對HKRBC的影響Key Rule / Section
1. Duration Matching 久期匹配
Keep asset dollar duration ≈ liability dollar duration 		Duration Factor (DF) → Application Ratio (AR) → MA ↑
If DF = 1.0 (perfect match): full adjusted spread is applied to MA
If DF = 0.5 (half-matched): MA is halved
Also: Interest Rate PCR ↓ because ΔNAVasset ≈ ΔNAVliability under IR stress 		Sch. 5 §6–8 (AR / DF)
Rule 47 (IR PCR)
2. Asset Quality (Credit Rating) 資產信用質量
Hold investment-grade eligible assets (bands 1–3) 		Lower RC% → higher adjusted spread → MA ↑
Higher-rated assets have lower risk correction (Band 1: RC%=11%, Band 3: RC%=20%)
Also: Credit Spread PCR ↓ because lower stressed spread widening factor applies to high-grade bonds 		Sch. 5 §5 Table 4 (RC%)
Rule 48 (CS PCR)
3. Eligible Asset Proportion 合資格資產佔比
Maximize eligible assets (bonds, ABS, loans) vs non-eligible (equities, derivatives) 		Eligible asset percentage (EA%) ↑ → DF cap ↑ → AR ↑ → MA ↑
EA% is capped at 100%; DF is min(EA%, asset$/liab$)
Non-eligible assets contribute 0 to MA but still bear equity / property risk PCR 		Sch. 5 §8 (EA%)
4. Cash Flow Matching 現金流匹配
Asset cash flows cover liability outflows in each period (no gaps) 		Accumulated cash flow shortfall % (ACS%) ↓ → Predictability Factor (PF) ↑ → AR ↑ → MA ↑
If ACS% ≥ 100% in any scenario: PF = 0, entire AR = 0 (no MA from spread)
Three scenarios tested: Base, Lapse Up, Mass Lapse 		Sch. 5 §7 (PF / ACS%)
5. Equity & Property Allocation 股票/房產配置
Hold some equity / property within a physically segregated Par fund 		Qualified LTA = 100 bps × equity & property proportion → additional MA component
Only available for Par / UL funds meeting Rule 24(3) criteria (physical segregation + GL34)
Equity PCR (Rule 49) still applies; net benefit depends on MA gain vs PCR cost 		Rule 24(3); Sch. 5 §10 (LTA)
6. Management Actions (FDB) 管理行動（酌情利益）
Documented policy on bonus / crediting rate adjustments under stress 		LAC(FDB) = reduction in PV(FDB) achievable under stress → reduces PCA
Must be realistic, pre-approved, and consistent with actual practice
ALM framework provides the evidence base for credible management actions 		Rule 43 (LAC FDB)
7. Rebalancing & Buy-and-Hold Strategy 買入並持有策略
Under MA, assets are assumed held to maturity (no active trading) 		ACS% calculation assumes no asset sales/purchases — cash flows are fixed
Active rebalancing invalidates MA eligibility for the affected portfolio
Investment strategy must align with ALM buy-and-hold discipline 		Sch. 5 §7(5)(a)

§11.1 Interest Rate Risk PCR — Duration Mismatch Mechanics 利率風險PCR與久期錯配

利率上行的兩個反向效應（ALM 核心矛盾）

效應方向機制時間窗口
MtM 損失（存量債券）負面 ↓現有債券市值下跌：ΔP ≈ −D × Δr
（例：7yr × +1% = −7%）		即時發生
Reinvestment Rate 上升（新現金流）正面 ↑到期本息再投資、新保費可買更高息債券逐步累積（數年）

淨效應取決於持有期 vs 資產久期：
• 短期（< 資產久期）：MtM 損失先行，reinvestment 未積累 → 淨效果為負
• 長期（> 資產久期）：reinvestment 持續積累，最終超過 MtM 損失 → 淨效果轉正
• 久期匹配點：資產久期 = 負債久期時，兩個效應在負債兌付時間窗口內恰好抵消 → 利率中性（ALM 目標）

對 Par Fund 的影響： 利率上行短期壓低 earned rate → credited rate 跟不上 → DPB lapse 上升（β₁ 觸發）； 但 2–3 年後 reinvestment rate 回升，earned rate 修復。 低利率才是 Par Fund 的長期風險（guaranteed rate > earned rate 持續無法靠 reinvestment 修復） → 這是 TVOG 主要由低利率情景驅動的原因。

HTM vs MtM — 會計分類 vs HKRBC EBS 處理

HTM（持有至到期，攤余成本）FVTPL / FVOCI（公允價值）
HKFRS 9
資產賬面		 攤余成本，不反映利率變動 → P&L 穩定 即時反映 MtM：FVTPL → 進 P&L；FVOCI → 進 OCI（不影響 P&L）
IFRS 17
負債賬面		 ⚠️ 錯配風險：IFRS 17 負債按當期貼現率計量，利率下行時負債上升；但 HTM 資產賬面不動 → P&L 出現大幅波動

緩解方法：採用 IFRS 17 OCI Option（保險財務收益/費用走 OCI）可部分對沖，但需資產也分類為 FVOCI		 ✅ 較好匹配：FVOCI 資產 + IFRS 17 OCI Option → 利率變動同時影響資產 OCI 和負債 OCI，P&L 相對穩定

FVTPL 資產則資產 MtM 進 P&L，但負債若走 OCI → 仍有 P&L 錯配
IFRS 17
貼現率		 IFRS 17 貼現率 = 反映負債現金流特性的當期利率（含流動性溢價），≠ HKRBC EBS 貼現率（無風險利率 + MA）。兩者可存在顯著差異，產生 IFRS 17 與 HKRBC 之間的雙重報告差異。
實際經濟價值 完全相同！利率上行時，兩者的經濟價值都下跌 ΔP ≈ −D × Δr，與會計分類無關
HKRBC EBS 全部按市場價值計量（Rule 11–12），與 HKFRS 9 / IFRS 17 分類無關 → HTM 在 EBS 下同樣 MtM
流動性風險 ⚠️ 更高：HKFRS 9 "tainting rule"，賣出任一 HTM 債券 → 整個 HTM 組合須重分類為 FVTPL/FVOCI 隨時可賣，但 FVTPL 實現賬面虧損進 P&L
術語說明：
• FVTPL（Fair Value Through Profit or Loss，公允價值計入損益）：資產每期按市值重估，MtM 盈虧直接進當期 P&L（損益表），影響當期利潤。利率波動 → 利潤波動大。
• FVOCI（Fair Value Through Other Comprehensive Income，公允價值計入其他綜合收益）：資產同樣按市值重估，但 MtM 盈虧先進 OCI（其他綜合收益），不影響當期 P&L；待資產出售時才「回收」進 P&L。利率波動 → P&L 相對穩定，但 OCI（淨資產）仍然波動。
• HTM（Held-to-Maturity，持有至到期）：按攤余成本入賬，完全不反映市值變動。P&L 最穩定，但流動性最差（tainting rule）。
• OCI（Other Comprehensive Income）：財務報表中介於 P&L 和股東權益之間的緩衝層，記錄未實現的公允價值變動，最終匯入「累計其他綜合收益」（AOCI），屬於股東權益的一部分。

關鍵理解：IFRS 17 並非讓 HTM 資產的變動反映在 P&L
IFRS 17 的核心改變是讓負債端按當期利率計量（不再像 IFRS 4 可以鎖定歷史利率）。 HTM 資產本身賬面依然不動——但負債在移動，造成單邊錯配：利率下行時，負債上升而資產賬面不變 → P&L 出現虧損。 這個虧損並非資產真正縮水，而是會計錯配製造的"假波動"。

資產 + 負債組合P&L淨資產 / OCI說明
HTM + IFRS 17⚠️ 大幅波動（負債動、資產不動）穩定最差組合：利率變動 → 負債漲、資產賬面不動 → 人為 P&L 虧損
FVOCI + IFRS 17 OCI Option✅ 穩定波動（利率敏感）最優組合：資產 MtM → OCI；負債變動 → OCI；雙邊對消，P&L 不受利率影響
FVTPL + IFRS 17（無 OCI Option）⚠️ 雙邊均進 P&L，波動但對稱穩定充分反映經濟現實，但 P&L 嘈雜；適合短期資產或交易型組合
HKRBC EBS無 P&L 概念全 MtM完全市場一致；資產負債均按市值，與會計分類無關

實務組合搭配： 多數壽險公司傾向 FVOCI + IFRS 17 OCI Option，使資產與負債的利率敏感性同時走 OCI，令 P&L 最穩定。 HTM 僅用於確實無需出售、且 IFRS 17 錯配可接受的短期或固定日期到期資產。

⛔ HTM 只是會計外觀上的平滑，不提供任何經濟保護。 ESG / ALM 建模必須用 MtM（經濟價值）建模，否則低估利率風險。

對 Par Fund earned rate 的影響：
• 正確做法（EBS 口徑）：earned rate 包含 MtM 變動，即使債券為 HTM 會計分類
• 常見簡化：部分公司的 AS 模型僅計算息票收入，忽略 HTM 組合的 MtM → 低估利率上行時的短期 earned rate 衝擊（及低利率時的 MtM 收益）

Under Rule 47, the Interest Rate (IR) stress scenarios apply parallel shifts to the yield curve. The resulting Net ΔNAV = ΔNAV(assets) − ΔNAV(liabilities) is the risk capital amount. The key ALM insight:

\[ \Delta\mathrm{NAV}_{\text{IR stress}} \approx (\mathcal{D}_{\text{assets}} - \mathcal{D}_{\text{liabilities}}) \times \Delta r \times V \]
If \(\mathcal{D}_{\text{assets}} = \mathcal{D}_{\text{liabilities}}\) → \(\Delta\mathrm{NAV} \approx 0\) → IR RCA \(\approx 0\)
ALM SituationIR PCR Impact 利率風險資本影響
Asset duration > Liability duration
(Assets longer than liabilities)		 IR Down stress hurts more: assets fall more than liabilities → large IR Down PCR
Common for life insurers with long-duration bonds backing shorter liabilities
Asset duration < Liability duration
(Assets shorter than liabilities)		 IR Up stress hurts more: liabilities fall less than assets → large IR Up PCR
Common when holding short-duration bonds vs long-tail liabilities
Asset duration ≈ Liability duration
(Well-matched)		 Both IR Up and IR Down PCR are small → lower total Market Risk PCR
MA recalculation (Rule 46) partially offsets: stressed MA reduces both asset and liability changes

§11.2 Credit Spread Risk & Dynamic MA 信用利差風險與動態匹配調整

Credit Spread (CS) stress under Rule 48 widens all eligible asset spreads. Crucially, under Rule 46, MA is recalculated under the CS stress — so the liability discount rate also falls, partially offsetting the asset value decline. This "Dynamic MA" effect is the key ALM benefit:

Dynamic MA under CS Stress:
Spread widening → asset prices fall ↓ (negative) & MA also rises ↑ (positive for liabilities)
Net PCRCS = ΔNAV(assets) − ΔNAV(liabilities with stressed MA)
The better the ALM match, the more the Dynamic MA offsets CS stress → lower CS PCR

§11.3 ALM Optimisation Summary — Capital vs Return Trade-off

Strategy 策略MA Benefit MA收益PCR Cost 資本成本Net Effect 整體效果
Extend duration to match liabilities DF ↑ → MA ↑ IR PCR ↓ ✅ Generally positive
Upgrade asset credit quality (e.g. BBB → A) RC% ↓ → adjusted spread ↑ but lower spread overall CS PCR ↓ Depends on spread income sacrifice
Add equity / property to Par fund Qualified LTA +100 bps (if eligible) Equity PCR ↑ Net positive only if LTA benefit > PCR cost
Improve cash flow matching (reduce ACS%) PF ↑ → MA ↑ May limit reinvestment flexibility ✅ Generally positive for capital
Strengthen FDB management action policy — LAC(FDB) ↑ → PCA ↓ ✅ Direct capital relief

§11.4 ALM Modelling Methodology Overview 建模方法論總覽

This section documents the five modelling building blocks required to implement HKRBC ALM in practice. Each block has well-defined inputs, algorithms, and output deliverables. Together they form the end-to-end pipeline from raw market data and policy data through to the final Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR).

End-to-End Modelling Pipeline 建模流水線 
  ┌──────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐    ┌─────────────┐    ┌─────────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐
  │ §9.1 Liab CF │    │ §9.2 Asset   │    │ §9.3 MA     │    │ §9.4 Stress &  │    │ §9.5 ESG     │
  │   Model      │ →  │   Valuation  │ →  │   Engine    │ →  │   PCR Engine    │ ←  │  (TVOG只用)   │
  │  (Prophet)   │    │  (OAS/MtM)   │    │  (Sch.5)    │    │  (Rule 37-86)   │    │              │
  └──────────────┘    └──────────────┘    └─────────────┘    └─────────────────┘    └──────────────┘
        ↓                    ↓                   ↓                    ↓                     ↓
  Guaranteed CF        Asset spread,        MA bps per          PCA (prescribed         Stochastic
  Mass Lapse CF        asset duration       portfolio           capital amount)         scenarios
  Lapse Up CF          per-bond CF                              CAR = CB / PCA          for TVOG

§11.5 Liability Cash Flow Model 負債現金流模型

目的 Purpose: Project all guaranteed and discretionary cash flows over the lifetime of the policy portfolio, segmented by currency × MA portfolio × Homogeneous Risk Group (HRG, Rule 53).

Building BlockMethodology & Key OutputsHKRBC Reference
Cash Flow Segmentation Split projected CFs into:
• Guaranteed CF (used in MA & ACS% calc)
• Discretionary CF (FDB) (used for LAC(FDB), TVOG)
• Premium CF (offsets liability CF in ACS%)
• Expense CF (allocated by region for Expense Risk) 		Rule 17, 18
Effective Duration Numerical derivative method (±1 bp twist):
$$D_{eff} = \frac{CE(r-\Delta) - CE(r+\Delta)}{2 \cdot CE(r) \cdot \Delta}$$ Must be computed under both RFR and RFR + MA discount rate (the latter for DF calc in MA engine). 		Sch. 5 §2
Lapse Scenario CFs Re-project under three scenarios:
• Base: Best estimate lapse rates
• Lapse Up: lapse × 1.40 (×1.40 to surrender rate)
• Mass Lapse: 30% one-off in Year 1
Output 3 sets of liability CFs (used by ACS% engine in §9.3). 		Rule 59; Sch. 5 §7
Dynamic Policyholder Behaviour Lapse / partial withdrawal / annuitisation rates may depend on (interest rate − crediting rate) gap.
Common functional form: lapse = base_lapse × (1 + β × (market_rate − credited_rate)) 		Rule 20
Management Actions Bonus / crediting rate adjustment rules under stress; required for LAC(FDB) credibility.
Must be documented in board-approved policy and applied consistently across scenarios. 		Rule 21, 43

Typical Implementation Stack

  • Commercial Models: Prophet (Sungard), AXIS (Moody's), RiskAgility FM (WTW), MG-ALFA (Milliman)
  • Run Time: 500K policies × 100 yrs × 5 currencies × 30+ scenarios ≈ 24-48 hours per quarter (parallel grid computing)
  • Output Format: Annual CF arrays per (scenario × MA portfolio × cash flow type), exported to CSV / Parquet

§11.6 Asset Valuation & Cash Flow Model 資產估值與現金流模型

目的 Purpose: Value each asset at market value, project its future cash flows, and derive the two key MA inputs: asset spread and asset duration.

AlgorithmMathematical SpecificationImplementation Notes
Asset Classification
(Sch. 5 §3 Table 1)		 Map each asset into:
• Eligible: Sovereign, Corporate, ABS, Other debt, Loans, Investment income receivable
• Non-eligible: Equity, Convertible bond (issuer/3P call), Derivative, Property, etc.
• Non-invested: Cash equivalent, affiliate investments, policy loans 		Lookup table keyed on instrument type code
Asset Spread (Z-Spread) Solver Solve numerically for z such that:
$$P_{dirty} = \sum_{t} \frac{CF_t}{(1 + r_t^{RFR} + z)^t}$$ Newton-Raphson: \(z_{n+1} = z_n - \frac{f(z_n)}{f'(z_n)}\) 		scipy.optimize.brentq or Newton; converges in 3-5 iterations
Asset Duration (Effective) Numerical derivative w.r.t. spread:
$$D_{asset} = \frac{P(z-1bp) - P(z+1bp)}{2 \cdot P(z) \cdot 1bp}$$ For callable bonds: cash flows depend on z (call exercise) — duration is reduced. 		Need price function that handles call schedule
Make-Whole Call (MWC) Spread Solve for MWC spread s such that:
Call Price = PV of remaining CF discounted at (Treasury rate + s)
Compare against Maximal MWC Spread (Sch. 5 §4 Table 2: 30/40/60/80 bps by rating). 		If solved s > cap: choose (a) redeem at first call date OR (b) partial MV recognition (ratio = X/Y)
ABS / MBS Modelling Prepayment model (PSA / SMM curve) + waterfall logic for tranching.
Output: prepayment-adjusted CFs, OAS, option-adjusted duration. 		Industry: Bloomberg YAS / Intex / RiskFront
Private Credit Valuation For illiquid assets: DCF + benchmark spread (similar issuer/rating).
Must justify "no observable market price" exception under Rule 34. 		Internal pricing committee approval required

Why This Step Matters for MA

Asset spread and asset duration are direct inputs to Schedule 5 §5 (adjusted spread) and §8 (DF / asset dollar duration). Wrong spread by 5 bps → MA off by ~3-4 bps → can swing capital ratio by 2-5 percentage points for a large insurer. This is why the IA requires spread validation against multiple data sources (Bloomberg, ICE, broker quotes).

§11.7 MA Engine 匹配調整引擎 (Schedule 5 Implementation)

目的 Purpose: Combine asset and liability inputs to compute the final MA (bps) per portfolio. This is the technical core of HKRBC modelling.

End-to-End Algorithm 完整算法步驟

⚠️ Key Implementation Challenge: MA is an implicit solution — it appears on both sides of the equation: MA → affects liability discount → affects liability dollar duration → affects DF → affects MA. Requires fixed-point iteration (Sch. 5 §8(2) allows ±10 bps tolerance between estimated and final MA).
StepCalculationInputs NeededSch. 5 Ref
1 For each eligible asset i:
adjusted_spreadi = asset_spreadi × (1 − RC%i)
where RC%i from §5 Table 4 by credit rating band 		From §9.2: asset_spread, credit rating band §5
2 Weight: ωi = MVi × asset_durationi / Σj(MVj × asset_durationj)
Portfolio adjusted spread = Σ ωi × adjusted_spreadi 		From §9.2: MV, asset_duration per asset §5(2)
3 Compute ACS% for each scenario {Base, Lapse Up, Mass Lapse}:
For t = 1, ..., LLP:
  Excesst = Excesst-1 + Asset_CFt − Liab_Net_CFt + max(Excesst-1,0)×rt
  PV_Shortfallt = |Excesst| × Π(1+rs)-1 if Excess<0 else 0
ACS%scen = maxt(PV_Shortfall) / MV_Eligible 		Asset CF (§9.2), Liab CF for 3 scenarios (§9.1) §7
4 PF = max(0, 1 − max(ACS%Base, ACS%Lapse Up, ACS%Mass Lapse)) ACS% from Step 3 §6
5 Initial guess: MA0 = portfolio_adjusted_spread × 0.5 (or use last period MA)
Compute Liability Dollar Duration with discount = RFR + MA0
Compute Asset Dollar Duration
DF = min(EA%, A$D / L$D), floored at 0 		Eff. Duration (§9.1), MV (§9.2) §8
6 Application Ratio: AR = PF × DF
Adjusted Spread Component = AR × portfolio_adjusted_spread 		— §6
7 Weighted CPS = Σ ωi × (CPS_lookupi − RCi)
where CPS_lookup from §9 Table 5 by maturity bucket × rating band
CPS Component = weighted_CPS × PF × A$D / L$D 		From §9.2: maturity, rating per asset §9
8 Qualified LTA (only if Rule 24(3) met — physical segregation + Par/UL):
LTA = 100 bps × min(equity_proportion, cap) 		Asset MV by class (§9.2) §10
9 MAnew = AR × adjusted_spread + CPS_component + LTA
Check: |MAnew − MA0| < 10 bps?
If YES → DONE. If NO → set MA0 = MAnew, go back to Step 5. 		— §8(2)

Reference Python Pseudo-code

def ma_engine(assets, liabilities, scenarios, tol_bps=10, max_iter=20):
    # --- Step 1-2: Asset Side ---
    for a in assets.eligible:
        a.adj_spread = a.asset_spread * (1 - RC_TABLE[a.rating_band])
    w = np.array([a.mv * a.duration for a in assets.eligible])
    w /= w.sum()
    port_adj_spread = (w * np.array([a.adj_spread for a in assets.eligible])).sum()
    
    # --- Step 3-4: PF (only computed once, scenario CFs are fixed) ---
    acs = {scen: compute_acs(assets, liabilities[scen]) for scen in scenarios}
    pf = max(0, 1 - max(acs.values()))
    
    # --- Step 5-9: Fixed-point iteration on MA ---
    ma_old = port_adj_spread * 0.5  # initial guess
    for it in range(max_iter):
        l_dollar_dur = liabilities.base.dollar_duration(discount_rate=RFR + ma_old)
        a_dollar_dur = sum(a.mv * a.duration for a in assets.eligible)
        ea_pct = sum(a.mv for a in assets.eligible) / assets.invested_mv
        df = max(0, min(ea_pct, a_dollar_dur / l_dollar_dur))
        ar = pf * df
        
        cps_weighted = sum(w[i] * (CPS_TABLE[a.bucket][a.rating_band] - RC_TABLE[a.rating_band])
                           for i, a in enumerate(assets.eligible))
        cps_comp = cps_weighted * pf * (a_dollar_dur / l_dollar_dur)
        
        lta = 100 if assets.qualifies_lta else 0
        lta *= min(assets.equity_proportion, assets.lta_cap)
        
        ma_new = ar * port_adj_spread + cps_comp + lta
        if abs(ma_new - ma_old) < tol_bps:
            return ma_new
        ma_old = ma_new
    raise ConvergenceError("MA did not converge within tolerance")

§11.8 Stress Scenario & PCR Engine 壓力測試與資本引擎

目的 Purpose: Apply each prescribed stress, compute ΔNAV = risk capital amount, aggregate using correlation matrices, and arrive at the final Prescribed Capital Amount (PCA).

Stress Scenario Inventory (30+ scenarios)

ModuleStressAsset Re-valMA Re-calcLiab Re-valRule
MarketIR Up parallel shift✓✓ Rule 46✓ (new MA + new RFR)47
IR Down parallel shift✓✓✓47
Credit Spread widening✓ (spread up)✓ (Dynamic MA, RC% unchanged)✓ via new MA48
Equity (+CCA)✓——49
Property, FX✓——50, 51
LifeMortality up——✓54
Longevity down——✓55
Life CAT (one-off)——✓56
Morbidity (4 sub-categories)——✓57
Expense (4 regions)——✓58
Lapse: Level / Trend / Mass——✓ max of three59
GIReserve / Premium / NatCatfactor-based, no full re-val——60-80
CDRCCounterparty defaultfactor × exposure——81
Op Risk—formula-based——86

Aggregation Algorithm 聚合算法

\[ \mathrm{RCA}_{\text{aggregate}} = \sqrt{\mathbf{w}^{\!\top} \Sigma\, \mathbf{w}} \qquad \Sigma = \text{correlation matrix (Sch. 7)},\quad \mathbf{w} = \text{RCA vector} \] \[ \mathrm{PCA} = \mathrm{RCA}_{\text{aggregate}} - \mathrm{LAC(FDB)} - \mathrm{LAC(DT)} + \mathrm{RCA}_{\text{OP}} \quad \text{\small Rule 37(1)} \]

Aggregation is done at two levels:

  1. Within module (e.g. Sch. 7 Table 3 for Market, Table 4 for Life) — combine sub-risks into module-level RCA
  2. Across modules (Sch. 7 Table 2) — combine Market / Life / GI / CDRC RCAs into RCAaggregate

LAC Engine 損失吸收引擎

ComponentAlgorithmReference
LAC(FDB) 1. Run base scenario: PV(FDB)base
2. Run aggregated stress: PV(FDB)stress (after management action reduces bonuses)
3. LAC(FDB) = max(PV(FDB)base − PV(FDB)stress, 0)
4. Cap at aggregate FDB reduction from base to minimum guaranteed; excess added back to PCA per Rule 37(1)(d) 		Rule 43
LAC(DT) 1. Compute stressed loss position
2. Apply effective tax rate (capped at statutory rate, e.g. 16.5% HK)
3. Tax saving = loss × effective tax rate
4. Must demonstrate realisability: future taxable income projection over reasonable horizon 		Rule 44

Appendix A: ESG Minimum Requirements Cap. 41R Rule 19(2) IA Circular 12 Jun 2024, Appendix 1

The Economic Scenario Generator (ESG) used in computing TVOG (Time Value of Options and Guarantees) must satisfy the following minimum requirements under Cap. 41R Rule 19(2):

  • Number of economic scenarios: ≥ 1,000
  • Time step: at least annual; more frequent steps required where product features are sensitive to cash flow timing
  • Must consider all material financial risks, including:
    • Interest rate model (at least short, medium and long tenor movements)
    • Fixed income asset return model (function of: level/change of interest rates, credit spreads, credit default, random component)
    • Inflation model (where relevant and material)
    • Equity asset return model
    • Cross-asset correlations 資產類別間相關性
  • Must pass Martingale Test 鞅檢驗 and Market Consistency Test
  • Must pass Implied Volatility Test — simulated asset class volatilities must be consistent with market data
  • Must validate simulated correlations against historical data
  • Calibrated to RFR yield curves prescribed in Cap. 41R Schedules 4 & 5 (with MA if applicable)
  • Must be re-calibrated at least annually 每年重新校準
